Um, hi. Yeah, thanks. I guess... well, what made me actually ask for help is that I barely want to eat anymore. Like, I'll be on my lunch break at the store, just staring at food I used to really like, and I end up throwing most of it away. It's weird because the breakroom at work always smells like microwave popcorn. Do you know that smell? It's like, burnt butter and old plastic. My manager, Dave, he always burns it. Every single Tuesday. He says it's because the microwave is broken, but I told him last month that the dial is just sticky. Nobody listens to me there anyway, they just keep burning the popcorn and leaving the door open so the whole back of the store smells like it... but yeah, I just look at my lunch and toss it.

Yeah, I guess so. Like, I used to get really excited about things. There's this gallery night downtown I'd always go to, and I used to play basketball with some guys from the neighborhood. We'd always hit up this food truck for tacos after. The guy who runs the truck, Hector, he makes this insane salsa verde. He said the recipe is from his grandmother in Michoacán, but I swear he puts something extra in it, like maybe a little bit of pineapple? I asked him once, but he just winked at me. He's always parked near the old fire station, which is getting torn down next month, I think. They're supposed to build condos there, which is stupid because the traffic on that street is already a nightmare. I tried to go to a city council meeting about it, but the bus was late... 

But yeah, I used to do all that, or even just help the neighborhood kids with their art stuff. Now, if I even bother showing up, I just stand there feeling completely flat. I don't care about the game, I don't want the tacos, and I usually just make up some excuse to leave early. It's just... nothing feels like anything anymore.

I'd say... probably about two months? Yeah, it's been right around two months. It started right after I got this window display award at the store, and got all this attention from the district manager. 

Which, honestly, was super weird because I didn't even want to do the display in the first place. Sarah usually does them, but she went on maternity leave. She had a little boy, I think. Or maybe a girl? I don't know, I saw a picture on her Facebook and the baby was wearing this bright yellow onesie that looked like a duck, complete with the little orange beak on the hood. But yeah, the district manager came down to the store—this guy Mr. Wallace, he drives this massive white SUV that always takes up two parking spaces right by the front doors. He came in wearing these really shiny leather shoes that squeaked on our linoleum floors and made a huge deal out of the display, taking pictures of me for the company newsletter. The newsletter is completely useless anyway, it's printed on this really cheap paper that leaves black ink all over your fingers if you hold it for more than a minute. I had to wash my hands like three times that day because I was working the register and didn't want to get ink on people's receipts. 

But yeah, ever since all that happened with him and the award... that's when I started feeling like this.

Like, I'll be on my lunch break at the store, just staring at food I used to really like, and I end up throwing most of it away. It's weird because the breakroom at work always smells like microwave popcorn. Do you know that smell? It's like, burnt butter and old plastic. My manager, Dave, he always burns it. Every single Tuesday. He says it's because the microwave is broken, but I told him last month that the dial is just sticky. Nobody listens to me there anyway, they just keep burning the popcorn and leaving the door open so the whole back of the store smells like it... but yeah, I just look at my lunch and toss it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you don't enjoy things as much as you used to, like hobbies or activities you once looked forward to?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess so. He's always parked near the old fire station, which is getting torn down next month, I think. But yeah, the district manager came down to the store—this guy Mr. Wallace, he drives this massive white SUV that always takes up two parking spaces right by the front doors. He came in wearing these really shiny leather shoes that squeaked on our linoleum floors and made a huge deal out of the display, taking pictures of me for the company newsletter. The newsletter is completely useless anyway, it's printed on this really cheap paper that leaves black ink all over your fingers if you hold it for more than a minute. I had to wash my hands like three times that day because I was working the register and didn't want to get ink on people's receipts. \n\nBut yeah, ever since all that happened with him and the award... that's when I started feeling like this.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you felt this way most of the day, nearly every day, since it started two months ago?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. I mean... I don't really know if it's every single day, or all day long. I haven't exactly been keeping a log or anything. It's really hard to keep track of time at the store anyway because the big clock above the registers is always exactly seven minutes fast. Brenda, she's the shift lead on weekdays, she completely refuses to climb up on the step-stool to fix it. She says leaving it fast helps the cashiers close out their tills quicker at the end of the night, but it really just messes up my bus schedule. 

I take the 42 down Miller Avenue to get home, and if it even drizzles, that bus is always running behind anyway. The driver, this older guy with a really thick gray mustache, he always blasts talk radio the whole ride. It's usually just static half the time because the antenna on the roof of the bus is bent completely backward. I think it hit a tree branch over by the park, the one where the city is supposed to be trimming the oaks but they never do. So you just sit there listening to static for forty minutes. 

But yeah, I don't really know about how often I feel this way. The days just kind of blur together.

Like, I used to get really excited about things. There's this gallery night downtown I'd always go to, and I used to play basketball with some guys from the neighborhood. The newsletter is completely useless anyway, it's printed on this really cheap paper that leaves black ink all over your fingers if you hold it for more than a minute. I had to wash my hands like three times that day because I was working the register and didn't want to get ink on people's receipts. \n\nBut yeah, ever since all that happened with him and the award... that's when I started feeling like this.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt this way most of the day, nearly every day, since it started two months ago?\n\nPatient: I mean... I don't really know if it's every single day, or all day long. I haven't exactly been keeping a log or anything. Brenda, she's the shift lead on weekdays, she completely refuses to climb up on the step-stool to fix it. She says leaving it fast helps the cashiers close out their tills quicker at the end of the night, but it really just messes up my bus schedule. \n\nI take the 42 down Miller Avenue to get home, and if it even drizzles, that bus is always running behind anyway. The driver, this older guy with a really thick gray mustache, he always blasts talk radio the whole ride. It's usually just static half the time because the antenna on the roof of the bus is bent completely backward. I think it hit a tree branch over by the park, the one where the city is supposed to be trimming the oaks but they never do. Yeah, I guess so. I just... I keep thinking I really didn't deserve that window display award. Or the chance to be a supervisor, which Mr. Wallace brought up when he was taking those pictures. I ended up parking next to this rusted-out blue sedan that looked like it had been sitting there since the nineties. It had one of those old cassette tape adapters hanging out of the dashboard. I haven't seen one of those since I was a kid. My dad used to have one in his truck, and we'd listen to these old mix tapes he made... 

But yeah, I just feel this heavy guilt all the time, like I somehow tricked everyone into thinking I'm good at my job. People at the store tell me I didn't trick anyone and that the display looks great, but I just feel like a total fraud.

I haven't seen one of those since I was a kid. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah, definitely. It's like... by the middle of my shift, my body just feels so heavy. Even doing simple stuff, like straightening the clothing racks or walking back to the stockroom, it just takes way more out of me than it should. 

And the stockroom is a nightmare anyway, it's so cramped back there. The delivery guys just leave the boxes wherever they want, usually right in front of the breakroom door. Last week they left this huge stack of winter coats right by the microwave, and someone spilled coffee on one of the boxes. It was that cheap hazelnut coffee they buy for the staff room, the kind that comes in those giant plastic tubs. It smells so artificial, kind of like a candle my aunt used to have in her bathroom. She lived in this tiny apartment above a bakery, and the whole place always smelled like yeast and fake hazelnut. She had these really creepy porcelain dolls lined up on a shelf, too... 

But yeah, I'm just exhausted all the time now. It's like I'm dragging myself through mud.

We'd always hit up this food truck for tacos after. The guy who runs the truck, Hector, he makes this insane salsa verde. He said the recipe is from his grandmother in Michoacán, but I swear he puts something extra in it, like maybe a little bit of pineapple? I asked him once, but he just winked at me. He's always parked near the old fire station, which is getting torn down next month, I think. They're supposed to build condos there, which is stupid because the traffic on that street is already a nightmare. I tried to go to a city council meeting about it, but the bus was late... \n\nBut yeah, I used to do all that, or even just help the neighborhood kids with their art stuff. Now, if I even bother showing up, I just stand there feeling completely flat. I don't care about the game, I don't want the tacos, and I usually just make up some excuse to leave early. It's just... nothing feels like anything anymore.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I'd say... probably about two months? Yeah, it's been right around two months. It started right after I got this window display award at the store, and got all this attention from the district manager. \n\nWhich, honestly, was super weird because I didn't even want to do the display in the first place. Sarah usually does them, but she went on maternity leave. She had a little boy, I think. Or maybe a girl? I don't know, I saw a picture on her Facebook and the baby was wearing this bright yellow onesie that looked like a duck, complete with the little orange beak on the hood. But yeah, the district manager came down to the store—this guy Mr. Wallace, he drives this massive white SUV that always takes up two parking spaces right by the front doors. He came in wearing these really shiny leather shoes that squeaked on our linoleum floors and made a huge deal out of the display, taking pictures of me for the company newsletter. The newsletter is completely useless anyway, it's printed on this really cheap paper that leaves black ink all over your fingers if you hold it for more than a minute. I had to wash my hands like three times that day because I was working the register and didn't want to get ink on people's receipts. \n\nBut yeah, ever since all that happened with him and the award... that's when I started feeling like this.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt this way most of the day, nearly every day, since it started two months ago?\n\nPatient: I mean... I don't really know if it's every single day, or all day long. I haven't exactly been keeping a log or anything. It's really hard to keep track of time at the store anyway because the big clock above the registers is always exactly seven minutes fast. Brenda, she's the shift lead on weekdays, she completely refuses to climb up on the step-stool to fix it. She says leaving it fast helps the cashiers close out their tills quicker at the end of the night, but it really just messes up my bus schedule. \n\nI take the 42 down Miller Avenue to get home, and if it even drizzles, that bus is always running behind anyway. The driver, this older guy with a really thick gray mustache, he always blasts talk radio the whole ride. It's usually just static half the time because the antenna on the roof of the bus is bent completely backward. I think it hit a tree branch over by the park, the one where the city is supposed to be trimming the oaks but they never do. So you just sit there listening to static for forty minutes. \n\nBut yeah, I don't really know about how often I feel this way. The days just kind of blur together.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like you’re not good enough, or been blaming yourself for things more than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess so. I just... I keep thinking I really didn't deserve that window display award. Or the chance to be a supervisor, which Mr. Wallace brought up when he was taking those pictures. He was talking about putting me in the management training program, which is held at the regional office out by the interstate. I've only been out there once, to drop off some paperwork for Brenda, and the parking lot is a total maze. They have these little numbered spots, but the paint is all peeling off, so you can't even tell if you're in a visitor spot or an employee spot. I ended up parking next to this rusted-out blue sedan that looked like it had been sitting there since the nineties. It had one of those old cassette tape adapters hanging out of the dashboard. I haven't seen one of those since I was a kid. My dad used to have one in his truck, and we'd listen to these old mix tapes he made... \n\nBut yeah, I just feel this heavy guilt all the time, like I somehow tricked everyone into thinking I'm good at my job. People at the store tell me I didn't trick anyone and that the display looks great, but I just feel like a total fraud.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt unusually tired or noticed a decrease in your energy levels most of the time since this started?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. It's like... by the middle of my shift, my body just feels so heavy. Even doing simple stuff, like straightening the clothing racks or walking back to the stockroom, it just takes way more out of me than it should. \n\nAnd the stockroom is a nightmare anyway, it's so cramped back there. The delivery guys just leave the boxes wherever they want, usually right in front of the breakroom door. Last week they left this huge stack of winter coats right by the microwave, and someone spilled coffee on one of the boxes. It was that cheap hazelnut coffee they buy for the staff room, the kind that comes in those giant plastic tubs. It smells so artificial, kind of like a candle my aunt used to have in her bathroom. She lived in this tiny apartment above a bakery, and the whole place always smelled like yeast and fake hazelnut. She had these really creepy porcelain dolls lined up on a shelf, too... \n\nBut yeah, I'm just exhausted all the time now. It's like I'm dragging myself through mud.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced a loss of interest or pleasure in activities you used to enjoy, almost every day since this started?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess so. I used to really look forward to stuff, you know? Like, there's this gallery night downtown I always went to, and I used to play basketball on the weekends with some guys from the neighborhood. We'd usually hit up this food truck after, the one that parks on 4th Street? They have these amazing al pastor tacos. He painted the whole thing himself, bright yellow with these little chili peppers along the side. But the paint is already starting to chip near the serving window because people always lean on it while they're waiting for their food. I told him he should put some clear coat over it, but he says he doesn't have the time. He's always so busy prep-cooking the meat. He marinates it overnight in this huge plastic tub... \n\nBut lately, even if I go to the gallery or go to help out with the kids' art program at the community center, I just... I don't feel anything. I just stand there feeling completely flat, and I usually just make up some excuse to go home early. It's just not the same anymore.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Yeah, actually... that's kind of what made me finally ask for help. I just barely want to eat anymore. Like, I'll be on my lunch break at the store, just staring at food I used to really like, and I just end up throwing most of it away. 

I used to always get this Italian sub from the deli next to our strip mall. The guy who runs it, Sal, he puts this special pepper relish on it that he makes himself. He told me the recipe is from his grandmother back in New Jersey. He said she used to grow her own peppers in this tiny little garden behind her apartment building. He showed me a picture of it once on his phone. His phone screen was completely shattered, though, like he must have dropped it face-down on concrete or something. I don't know how he even reads his text messages through all those cracks. I dropped my phone once in a puddle outside the movie theater and the whole screen just went completely black. We were going to see that new action movie, but the line was wrapped all the way around the building, so we ended up just going to the arcade next door instead. They had one of those old pinball machines with the alien theme, the one where the bumpers light up green... 

But yeah, I just look at my lunch now and I don't want any of it. It just goes in the trash.

Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I haven't really noticed anything like that. I mean, my mom said I was walking a little slower the other day when we went to the grocery store, but I think I was just tired. The grocery store was so packed, too. It was a Sunday afternoon, and everyone was buying stuff for the big game. They had this huge display of tortilla chips right when you walk in, shaped like a football stadium. It was actually pretty cool, someone must have spent hours stacking all those bags. But then some kid ran his cart right into it and knocked like half of them down. The manager looked so mad, he was this tall guy with a really bad combover. He was wearing this bright red tie that totally clashed with his shirt. It reminded me of my old middle school principal, Mr. Henderson. He always wore these awful ties with little cartoon characters on them... 

But no, I don't think I'm moving slower or anything. And I'm definitely not restless or fidgety. I'm too tired to be restless.

He marinates it overnight in this huge plastic tub... \n\nBut lately, even if I go to the gallery or go to help out with the kids' art program at the community center, I just... I don't feel anything. I just stand there feeling completely flat, and I usually just make up some excuse to go home early. It's just not the same anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced a decrease in appetite or noticed any significant weight loss since this started?\n\nPatient: Yeah, actually... that's kind of what made me finally ask for help. I just barely want to eat anymore. Like, I'll be on my lunch break at the store, just staring at food I used to really like, and I just end up throwing most of it away. \n\nI used to always get this Italian sub from the deli next to our strip mall. The guy who runs it, Sal, he puts this special pepper relish on it that he makes himself. He told me the recipe is from his grandmother back in New Jersey. He said she used to grow her own peppers in this tiny little garden behind her apartment building. He showed me a picture of it once on his phone. His phone screen was completely shattered, though, like he must have dropped it face-down on concrete or something. I don't know how he even reads his text messages through all those cracks. I dropped my phone once in a puddle outside the movie theater and the whole screen just went completely black. We were going to see that new action movie, but the line was wrapped all the way around the building, so we ended up just going to the arcade next door instead. They had one of those old pinball machines with the alien theme, the one where the bumpers light up green... \n\nBut yeah, I just look at my lunch now and I don't want any of it. It just goes in the trash.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving or speaking more slowly than usual, or on the flip side, feeling restless or fidgety more than usual?\n\nPatient: I haven't really noticed anything like that. It was actually pretty cool, someone must have spent hours stacking all those bags. But then some kid ran his cart right into it and knocked like half of them down. It reminded me of my old middle school principal, Mr. Henderson. He always wore these awful ties with little cartoon characters on them... \n\nBut no, I don't think I'm moving slower or anything. And I'm definitely not restless or fidgety. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I don't know if I'd say most of the day... I mean, I guess I have my moments, but I wouldn't say it's all the time. Sometimes I feel okay when I'm at work, at least until it gets really slow. When the store is dead, time just drags, you know? We had this one shift last Tuesday where I think we only had like three customers the entire afternoon. The maintenance guy, Dave, said he put in a work order to fix them, but corporate takes forever to approve anything. He showed me the paperwork he submitted, and it was like five pages long just to change a light fixture... 

But yeah, I don't think I'm depressed all day every day. Just... sometimes.

Nobody listens to me there anyway, they just keep burning the popcorn and leaving the door open so the whole back of the store smells like it... but yeah, I just look at my lunch and toss it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you don't enjoy things as much as you used to, like hobbies or activities you once looked forward to?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess so. Like, I used to get really excited about things. There's this gallery night downtown I'd always go to, and I used to play basketball with some guys from the neighborhood. We'd always hit up this food truck for tacos after. The guy who runs the truck, Hector, he makes this insane salsa verde. He said the recipe is from his grandmother in Michoacán, but I swear he puts something extra in it, like maybe a little bit of pineapple? I asked him once, but he just winked at me. He's always parked near the old fire station, which is getting torn down next month, I think. They're supposed to build condos there, which is stupid because the traffic on that street is already a nightmare. I tried to go to a city council meeting about it, but the bus was late... \n\nBut yeah, I used to do all that, or even just help the neighborhood kids with their art stuff. Now, if I even bother showing up, I just stand there feeling completely flat. I don't care about the game, I don't want the tacos, and I usually just make up some excuse to leave early. It's just... nothing feels like anything anymore.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I'd say... probably about two months? Yeah, it's been right around two months. It started right after I got this window display award at the store, and got all this attention from the district manager. \n\nWhich, honestly, was super weird because I didn't even want to do the display in the first place. Sarah usually does them, but she went on maternity leave. She had a little boy, I think. Or maybe a girl? I don't know, I saw a picture on her Facebook and the baby was wearing this bright yellow onesie that looked like a duck, complete with the little orange beak on the hood. But yeah, the district manager came down to the store—this guy Mr. Wallace, he drives this massive white SUV that always takes up two parking spaces right by the front doors. He came in wearing these really shiny leather shoes that squeaked on our linoleum floors and made a huge deal out of the display, taking pictures of me for the company newsletter. The newsletter is completely useless anyway, it's printed on this really cheap paper that leaves black ink all over your fingers if you hold it for more than a minute. I had to wash my hands like three times that day because I was working the register and didn't want to get ink on people's receipts. \n\nBut yeah, ever since all that happened with him and the award... that's when I started feeling like this.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt this way most of the day, nearly every day, since it started two months ago?\n\nPatient: I mean... I don't really know if it's every single day, or all day long. I haven't exactly been keeping a log or anything. It's really hard to keep track of time at the store anyway because the big clock above the registers is always exactly seven minutes fast. Brenda, she's the shift lead on weekdays, she completely refuses to climb up on the step-stool to fix it. She says leaving it fast helps the cashiers close out their tills quicker at the end of the night, but it really just messes up my bus schedule. \n\nI take the 42 down Miller Avenue to get home, and if it even drizzles, that bus is always running behind anyway. The driver, this older guy with a really thick gray mustache, he always blasts talk radio the whole ride. It's usually just static half the time because the antenna on the roof of the bus is bent completely backward. I think it hit a tree branch over by the park, the one where the city is supposed to be trimming the oaks but they never do. So you just sit there listening to static for forty minutes. \n\nBut yeah, I don't really know about how often I feel this way. The days just kind of blur together.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like you’re not good enough, or been blaming yourself for things more than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess so. I just... I keep thinking I really didn't deserve that window display award. Or the chance to be a supervisor, which Mr. Wallace brought up when he was taking those pictures. He was talking about putting me in the management training program, which is held at the regional office out by the interstate. I've only been out there once, to drop off some paperwork for Brenda, and the parking lot is a total maze. They have these little numbered spots, but the paint is all peeling off, so you can't even tell if you're in a visitor spot or an employee spot. I ended up parking next to this rusted-out blue sedan that looked like it had been sitting there since the nineties. It had one of those old cassette tape adapters hanging out of the dashboard. I haven't seen one of those since I was a kid. My dad used to have one in his truck, and we'd listen to these old mix tapes he made... \n\nBut yeah, I just feel this heavy guilt all the time, like I somehow tricked everyone into thinking I'm good at my job. People at the store tell me I didn't trick anyone and that the display looks great, but I just feel like a total fraud.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt unusually tired or noticed a decrease in your energy levels most of the time since this started?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. It's like... by the middle of my shift, my body just feels so heavy. Even doing simple stuff, like straightening the clothing racks or walking back to the stockroom, it just takes way more out of me than it should. \n\nAnd the stockroom is a nightmare anyway, it's so cramped back there. The delivery guys just leave the boxes wherever they want, usually right in front of the breakroom door. Last week they left this huge stack of winter coats right by the microwave, and someone spilled coffee on one of the boxes. It was that cheap hazelnut coffee they buy for the staff room, the kind that comes in those giant plastic tubs. It smells so artificial, kind of like a candle my aunt used to have in her bathroom. She lived in this tiny apartment above a bakery, and the whole place always smelled like yeast and fake hazelnut. She had these really creepy porcelain dolls lined up on a shelf, too... \n\nBut yeah, I'm just exhausted all the time now. I ended up parking next to this rusted-out blue sedan that looked like it had been sitting there since the nineties. It had one of those old cassette tape adapters hanging out of the dashboard. I haven't seen one of those since I was a kid. My dad used to have one in his truck, and we'd listen to these old mix tapes he made... \n\nBut yeah, I just feel this heavy guilt all the time, like I somehow tricked everyone into thinking I'm good at my job. People at the store tell me I didn't trick anyone and that the display looks great, but I just feel like a total fraud.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt unusually tired or noticed a decrease in your energy levels most of the time since this started?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. It's like... by the middle of my shift, my body just feels so heavy. Even doing simple stuff, like straightening the clothing racks or walking back to the stockroom, it just takes way more out of me than it should. \n\nAnd the stockroom is a nightmare anyway, it's so cramped back there. The delivery guys just leave the boxes wherever they want, usually right in front of the breakroom door. Last week they left this huge stack of winter coats right by the microwave, and someone spilled coffee on one of the boxes. It was that cheap hazelnut coffee they buy for the staff room, the kind that comes in those giant plastic tubs. It smells so artificial, kind of like a candle my aunt used to have in her bathroom. She lived in this tiny apartment above a bakery, and the whole place always smelled like yeast and fake hazelnut. She had these really creepy porcelain dolls lined up on a shelf, too... \n\nBut yeah, I'm just exhausted all the time now. It's like I'm dragging myself through mud.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced a loss of interest or pleasure in activities you used to enjoy, almost every day since this started?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess so. I used to really look forward to stuff, you know? Like, there's this gallery night downtown I always went to, and I used to play basketball on the weekends with some guys from the neighborhood. We'd usually hit up this food truck after, the one that parks on 4th Street? They have these amazing al pastor tacos. The guy who runs it, Hector, he actually used to work at a mechanic shop with my cousin before he bought the truck. He painted the whole thing himself, bright yellow with these little chili peppers along the side. But the paint is already starting to chip near the serving window because people always lean on it while they're waiting for their food. I told him he should put some clear coat over it, but he says he doesn't have the time. He's always so busy prep-cooking the meat. He marinates it overnight in this huge plastic tub... \n\nBut lately, even if I go to the gallery or go to help out with the kids' art program at the community center, I just... I don't feel anything. I just stand there feeling completely flat, and I usually just make up some excuse to go home early. It's just not the same anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced a decrease in appetite or noticed any significant weight loss since this started?\n\nPatient: Yeah, actually... that's kind of what made me finally ask for help. I just barely want to eat anymore. Like, I'll be on my lunch break at the store, just staring at food I used to really like, and I just end up throwing most of it away. \n\nI used to always get this Italian sub from the deli next to our strip mall. The guy who runs it, Sal, he puts this special pepper relish on it that he makes himself. He told me the recipe is from his grandmother back in New Jersey. He said she used to grow her own peppers in this tiny little garden behind her apartment building. He showed me a picture of it once on his phone. His phone screen was completely shattered, though, like he must have dropped it face-down on concrete or something. I don't know how he even reads his text messages through all those cracks. I dropped my phone once in a puddle outside the movie theater and the whole screen just went completely black. We were going to see that new action movie, but the line was wrapped all the way around the building, so we ended up just going to the arcade next door instead. They had one of those old pinball machines with the alien theme, the one where the bumpers light up green... \n\nBut yeah, I just look at my lunch now and I don't want any of it. It just goes in the trash.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving or speaking more slowly than usual, or on the flip side, feeling restless or fidgety more than usual?\n\nPatient: I haven't really noticed anything like that. It was actually pretty cool, someone must have spent hours stacking all those bags. But then some kid ran his cart right into it and knocked like half of them down. The manager looked so mad, he was this tall guy with a really bad combover. He was wearing this bright red tie that totally clashed with his shirt. It reminded me of my old middle school principal, Mr. Henderson. He always wore these awful ties with little cartoon characters on them... \n\nBut no, I don't think I'm moving slower or anything. And I'm definitely not restless or fidgety. No, no, I haven't really thought about anything like that. I mean, I guess things feel pretty pointless sometimes, but I wouldn't actually do anything to hurt myself. It's just... They're supposed to build condos there, which is stupid because the traffic on that street is already a nightmare. Sarah usually does them, but she went on maternity leave. She had a little boy, I think. Or maybe a girl? I don't know, I saw a picture on her Facebook and the baby was wearing this bright yellow onesie that looked like a duck, complete with the little orange beak on the hood. The newsletter is completely useless anyway, it's printed on this really cheap paper that leaves black ink all over your fingers if you hold it for more than a minute. I had to wash my hands like three times that day because I was working the register and didn't want to get ink on people's receipts. \n\nBut yeah, ever since all that happened with him and the award... that's when I started feeling like this.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt this way most of the day, nearly every day, since it started two months ago?\n\nPatient: I mean... I don't really know if it's every single day, or all day long. I haven't exactly been keeping a log or anything. Brenda, she's the shift lead on weekdays, she completely refuses to climb up on the step-stool to fix it. She says leaving it fast helps the cashiers close out their tills quicker at the end of the night, but it really just messes up my bus schedule. \n\nI take the 42 down Miller Avenue to get home, and if it even drizzles, that bus is always running behind anyway. The driver, this older guy with a really thick gray mustache, he always blasts talk radio the whole ride. It's usually just static half the time because the antenna on the roof of the bus is bent completely backward. I think it hit a tree branch over by the park, the one where the city is supposed to be trimming the oaks but they never do. So you just sit there listening to static for forty minutes. \n\nBut yeah, I don't really know about how often I feel this way. I haven't seen one of those since I was a kid. Last week they left this huge stack of winter coats right by the microwave, and someone spilled coffee on one of the boxes. It was that cheap hazelnut coffee they buy for the staff room, the kind that comes in those giant plastic tubs. It smells so artificial, kind of like a candle my aunt used to have in her bathroom. She lived in this tiny apartment above a bakery, and the whole place always smelled like yeast and fake hazelnut. She had these really creepy porcelain dolls lined up on a shelf, too... \n\nBut yeah, I'm just exhausted all the time now. It's like I'm dragging myself through mud.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced a loss of interest or pleasure in activities you used to enjoy, almost every day since this started?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess so. The guy who runs it, Hector, he actually used to work at a mechanic shop with my cousin before he bought the truck. We had this one shift last Tuesday where I think we only had like three customers the entire afternoon. One of them was this lady trying to return a blender without a receipt. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Didn't you just ask me that? I mean, I think I said I don't feel like that all the time. Just sometimes. It's like... I don't know, it's like the weather. Sometimes it's cloudy, sometimes it's not. Speaking of the weather, it's supposed to rain all weekend, which really sucks because my buddy Mark wanted to go to this car show out by the fairgrounds. He's got this old '67 Mustang he's been fixing up for like, three years now. He finally got the engine rebuilt and painted it this really crazy metallic blue color. It looks awesome, but he's super paranoid about getting it wet. He won't even drive it if there's a puddle on the road. We went to grab burgers the other day, and he parked like a mile away from the restaurant just so no one would park next to him and ding his doors. Then, of course, it started drizzling while we were walking back, and he totally freaked out. He actually took off his jacket and tried to cover the hood with it. It was hilarious, but he was so mad at me for laughing... 

So yeah, like I said, not every day. Just off and on, you know?

The driver, this older guy with a really thick gray mustache, he always blasts talk radio the whole ride. It's usually just static half the time because the antenna on the roof of the bus is bent completely backward. I keep thinking I really didn't deserve that window display award. Or the chance to be a supervisor, which Mr. Wallace brought up when he was taking those pictures. He was talking about putting me in the management training program, which is held at the regional office out by the interstate. They have these little numbered spots, but the paint is all peeling off, so you can't even tell if you're in a visitor spot or an employee spot. I ended up parking next to this rusted-out blue sedan that looked like it had been sitting there since the nineties. It had one of those old cassette tape adapters hanging out of the dashboard. I haven't seen one of those since I was a kid. My dad used to have one in his truck, and we'd listen to these old mix tapes he made... \n\nBut yeah, I just feel this heavy guilt all the time, like I somehow tricked everyone into thinking I'm good at my job. People at the store tell me I didn't trick anyone and that the display looks great, but I just feel like a total fraud.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt unusually tired or noticed a decrease in your energy levels most of the time since this started?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. It's like... by the middle of my shift, my body just feels so heavy. I used to really look forward to stuff, you know? Like, there's this gallery night downtown I always went to, and I used to play basketball on the weekends with some guys from the neighborhood. We'd usually hit up this food truck after, the one that parks on 4th Street? They have these amazing al pastor tacos. The guy who runs it, Hector, he actually used to work at a mechanic shop with my cousin before he bought the truck. He painted the whole thing himself, bright yellow with these little chili peppers along the side. But the paint is already starting to chip near the serving window because people always lean on it while they're waiting for their food. I told him he should put some clear coat over it, but he says he doesn't have the time. He's always so busy prep-cooking the meat. He marinates it overnight in this huge plastic tub... \n\nBut lately, even if I go to the gallery or go to help out with the kids' art program at the community center, I just... I don't feel anything. I just stand there feeling completely flat, and I usually just make up some excuse to go home early. It's just not the same anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced a decrease in appetite or noticed any significant weight loss since this started?\n\nPatient: Yeah, actually... that's kind of what made me finally ask for help. I just barely want to eat anymore. Like, I'll be on my lunch break at the store, just staring at food I used to really like, and I just end up throwing most of it away. \n\nI used to always get this Italian sub from the deli next to our strip mall. The guy who runs it, Sal, he puts this special pepper relish on it that he makes himself. He told me the recipe is from his grandmother back in New Jersey. He said she used to grow her own peppers in this tiny little garden behind her apartment building. He showed me a picture of it once on his phone. His phone screen was completely shattered, though, like he must have dropped it face-down on concrete or something. I don't know how he even reads his text messages through all those cracks. I dropped my phone once in a puddle outside the movie theater and the whole screen just went completely black. We were going to see that new action movie, but the line was wrapped all the way around the building, so we ended up just going to the arcade next door instead. They had one of those old pinball machines with the alien theme, the one where the bumpers light up green... \n\nBut yeah, I just look at my lunch now and I don't want any of it. It just goes in the trash.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving or speaking more slowly than usual, or on the flip side, feeling restless or fidgety more than usual?\n\nPatient: I haven't really noticed anything like that. I mean, my mom said I was walking a little slower the other day when we went to the grocery store, but I think I was just tired. The grocery store was so packed, too. It was a Sunday afternoon, and everyone was buying stuff for the big game. They had this huge display of tortilla chips right when you walk in, shaped like a football stadium. It was actually pretty cool, someone must have spent hours stacking all those bags. But then some kid ran his cart right into it and knocked like half of them down. The manager looked so mad, he was this tall guy with a really bad combover. He was wearing this bright red tie that totally clashed with his shirt. It reminded me of my old middle school principal, Mr. Henderson. He always wore these awful ties with little cartoon characters on them... \n\nBut no, I don't think I'm moving slower or anything. And I'm definitely not restless or fidgety. I'm too tired to be restless.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt down or depressed most of the day, nearly every day, since this started two months ago?\n\nPatient: I don't know if I'd say most of the day... I mean, I guess I have my moments, but I wouldn't say it's all the time. Sometimes I feel okay when I'm at work, at least until it gets really slow. When the store is dead, time just drags, you know? We had this one shift last Tuesday where I think we only had like three customers the entire afternoon. One of them was this lady trying to return a blender without a receipt. She kept arguing with my manager, saying she bought it last week, but the box looked like it had been sitting in her garage for ten years. It was covered in dust and one of the corners was completely crushed. She had this tiny little dog in her purse, too, one of those Chihuahuas that just shakes constantly. It had a little rhinestone collar that kept catching the light from the fluorescent bulbs above the register. Those bulbs give me a headache sometimes, they have this constant low buzzing sound. The maintenance guy, Dave, said he put in a work order to fix them, but corporate takes forever to approve anything. He showed me the paperwork he submitted, and it was like five pages long just to change a light fixture... \n\nBut yeah, I don't think I'm depressed all day every day. Just... sometimes.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like life wasn’t worth living or had thoughts about ending your life since this started?\n\nPatient: No, no, I haven't really thought about anything like that. I mean, I guess things feel pretty pointless sometimes, but I wouldn't actually do anything to hurt myself. It's just... I don't know, it's hard to explain. Like when you're watching a movie and you already know how it ends, so you just kind of stop paying attention? I was watching this documentary the other night about deep-sea diving, and they were showing these crazy fish that live way down where there's no light at all. Some of them actually make their own light, like they have these little glowing things hanging off their heads to attract smaller fish. It's wild how things adapt like that. The guy narrating it had this really deep, soothing voice, almost like Morgan Freeman but not quite. I think he might have been British. My uncle used to do this fake British accent whenever he'd read me stories when I was a kid. He had this whole routine where he'd pretend to be a proper gentleman drinking tea, but he'd always end up spilling it on himself. He was pretty clumsy, actually. He broke his ankle once just stepping off a curb outside a diner... \n\nBut no, to answer your question, I haven't had any thoughts about ending my life. Just feeling kind of empty, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt down or sad most of the day, nearly every day, for the past two months?\n\nPatient: Didn't you just ask me that? I mean, I think I said I don't feel like that all the time. Just sometimes. It's like... I don't know, it's like the weather. Sometimes it's cloudy, sometimes it's not. Speaking of the weather, it's supposed to rain all weekend, which really sucks because my buddy Mark wanted to go to this car show out by the fairgrounds. He's got this old '67 Mustang he's been fixing up for like, three years now. He finally got the engine rebuilt and painted it this really crazy metallic blue color. Then, of course, it started drizzling while we were walking back, and he totally freaked out. He actually took off his jacket and tried to cover the hood with it. Two years? Um, no, I don't think so. Not for weeks at a time, anyway. Two years ago I was actually living in this really crappy apartment above a bakery. It wasn't sad, just... loud, you know? The bakers would get in at like 3 AM and start banging these giant metal trays around. But the smell was incredible, man. Fresh bread every morning. The owner, this guy named Sal, he was huge. Like, former football player huge. He used to give me the leftover bagels at the end of the day. Most of them were pretty stale by then, so I'd take them to the park down the street and throw them to the ducks. Except one time this massive goose chased me because I guess I didn't throw the bagel fast enough. Geese are so aggressive. They have those weird little teeth on their tongues, did you know that? I saw a picture of it online once and it totally freaked me out. I don't know why a bird needs teeth on its tongue, it just seems like overkill... 

But anyway, no, I haven't had anything like that going on for two years. Just recently, really.

Like, I'll be on my lunch break at the store, just staring at food I used to really like, and I end up throwing most of it away. It's weird because the breakroom at work always smells like microwave popcorn. Do you know that smell? It's like, burnt butter and old plastic. My manager, Dave, he always burns it. Every single Tuesday. He says it's because the microwave is broken, but I told him last month that the dial is just sticky. Nobody listens to me there anyway, they just keep burning the popcorn and leaving the door open so the whole back of the store smells like it... but yeah, I just look at my lunch and toss it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you don't enjoy things as much as you used to, like hobbies or activities you once looked forward to?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess so. Like, I used to get really excited about things. There's this gallery night downtown I'd always go to, and I used to play basketball with some guys from the neighborhood. We'd always hit up this food truck for tacos after. The guy who runs the truck, Hector, he makes this insane salsa verde. He said the recipe is from his grandmother in Michoacán, but I swear he puts something extra in it, like maybe a little bit of pineapple? I asked him once, but he just winked at me. He's always parked near the old fire station, which is getting torn down next month, I think. They're supposed to build condos there, which is stupid because the traffic on that street is already a nightmare. I tried to go to a city council meeting about it, but the bus was late... \n\nBut yeah, I used to do all that, or even just help the neighborhood kids with their art stuff. Now, if I even bother showing up, I just stand there feeling completely flat. I don't care about the game, I don't want the tacos, and I usually just make up some excuse to leave early. It's just... nothing feels like anything anymore.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I'd say... probably about two months? Yeah, it's been right around two months. It started right after I got this window display award at the store, and got all this attention from the district manager. \n\nWhich, honestly, was super weird because I didn't even want to do the display in the first place. Sarah usually does them, but she went on maternity leave. She had a little boy, I think. Or maybe a girl? I don't know, I saw a picture on her Facebook and the baby was wearing this bright yellow onesie that looked like a duck, complete with the little orange beak on the hood. But yeah, the district manager came down to the store—this guy Mr. Wallace, he drives this massive white SUV that always takes up two parking spaces right by the front doors. He came in wearing these really shiny leather shoes that squeaked on our linoleum floors and made a huge deal out of the display, taking pictures of me for the company newsletter. The newsletter is completely useless anyway, it's printed on this really cheap paper that leaves black ink all over your fingers if you hold it for more than a minute. I had to wash my hands like three times that day because I was working the register and didn't want to get ink on people's receipts. \n\nBut yeah, ever since all that happened with him and the award... that's when I started feeling like this.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt this way most of the day, nearly every day, since it started two months ago?\n\nPatient: I mean... I don't really know if it's every single day, or all day long. I haven't exactly been keeping a log or anything. It's really hard to keep track of time at the store anyway because the big clock above the registers is always exactly seven minutes fast. Brenda, she's the shift lead on weekdays, she completely refuses to climb up on the step-stool to fix it. She says leaving it fast helps the cashiers close out their tills quicker at the end of the night, but it really just messes up my bus schedule. \n\nI take the 42 down Miller Avenue to get home, and if it even drizzles, that bus is always running behind anyway. The driver, this older guy with a really thick gray mustache, he always blasts talk radio the whole ride. It's usually just static half the time because the antenna on the roof of the bus is bent completely backward. I think it hit a tree branch over by the park, the one where the city is supposed to be trimming the oaks but they never do. So you just sit there listening to static for forty minutes. \n\nBut yeah, I don't really know about how often I feel this way. The days just kind of blur together.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like you’re not good enough, or been blaming yourself for things more than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess so. I just... I keep thinking I really didn't deserve that window display award. Or the chance to be a supervisor, which Mr. Wallace brought up when he was taking those pictures. He was talking about putting me in the management training program, which is held at the regional office out by the interstate. I've only been out there once, to drop off some paperwork for Brenda, and the parking lot is a total maze. They have these little numbered spots, but the paint is all peeling off, so you can't even tell if you're in a visitor spot or an employee spot. I ended up parking next to this rusted-out blue sedan that looked like it had been sitting there since the nineties. It had one of those old cassette tape adapters hanging out of the dashboard. I haven't seen one of those since I was a kid. My dad used to have one in his truck, and we'd listen to these old mix tapes he made... \n\nBut yeah, I just feel this heavy guilt all the time, like I somehow tricked everyone into thinking I'm good at my job. People at the store tell me I didn't trick anyone and that the display looks great, but I just feel like a total fraud.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt unusually tired or noticed a decrease in your energy levels most of the time since this started?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. It's like... by the middle of my shift, my body just feels so heavy. Even doing simple stuff, like straightening the clothing racks or walking back to the stockroom, it just takes way more out of me than it should. \n\nAnd the stockroom is a nightmare anyway, it's so cramped back there. The delivery guys just leave the boxes wherever they want, usually right in front of the breakroom door. Last week they left this huge stack of winter coats right by the microwave, and someone spilled coffee on one of the boxes. It was that cheap hazelnut coffee they buy for the staff room, the kind that comes in those giant plastic tubs. It smells so artificial, kind of like a candle my aunt used to have in her bathroom. She lived in this tiny apartment above a bakery, and the whole place always smelled like yeast and fake hazelnut. She had these really creepy porcelain dolls lined up on a shelf, too... \n\nBut yeah, I'm just exhausted all the time now. It's like I'm dragging myself through mud.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced a loss of interest or pleasure in activities you used to enjoy, almost every day since this started?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess so. I used to really look forward to stuff, you know? Like, there's this gallery night downtown I always went to, and I used to play basketball on the weekends with some guys from the neighborhood. We'd usually hit up this food truck after, the one that parks on 4th Street? They have these amazing al pastor tacos. The guy who runs it, Hector, he actually used to work at a mechanic shop with my cousin before he bought the truck. He painted the whole thing himself, bright yellow with these little chili peppers along the side. But the paint is already starting to chip near the serving window because people always lean on it while they're waiting for their food. I told him he should put some clear coat over it, but he says he doesn't have the time. He's always so busy prep-cooking the meat. He marinates it overnight in this huge plastic tub... \n\nBut lately, even if I go to the gallery or go to help out with the kids' art program at the community center, I just... I don't feel anything. I just stand there feeling completely flat, and I usually just make up some excuse to go home early. It's just not the same anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced a decrease in appetite or noticed any significant weight loss since this started?\n\nPatient: Yeah, actually... that's kind of what made me finally ask for help. I just barely want to eat anymore. Like, I'll be on my lunch break at the store, just staring at food I used to really like, and I just end up throwing most of it away. \n\nI used to always get this Italian sub from the deli next to our strip mall. The guy who runs it, Sal, he puts this special pepper relish on it that he makes himself. He told me the recipe is from his grandmother back in New Jersey. He said she used to grow her own peppers in this tiny little garden behind her apartment building. He showed me a picture of it once on his phone. His phone screen was completely shattered, though, like he must have dropped it face-down on concrete or something. I don't know how he even reads his text messages through all those cracks. I dropped my phone once in a puddle outside the movie theater and the whole screen just went completely black. We were going to see that new action movie, but the line was wrapped all the way around the building, so we ended up just going to the arcade next door instead. They had one of those old pinball machines with the alien theme, the one where the bumpers light up green... \n\nBut yeah, I just look at my lunch now and I don't want any of it. It just goes in the trash.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving or speaking more slowly than usual, or on the flip side, feeling restless or fidgety more than usual?\n\nPatient: I haven't really noticed anything like that. I mean, my mom said I was walking a little slower the other day when we went to the grocery store, but I think I was just tired. The grocery store was so packed, too. It was a Sunday afternoon, and everyone was buying stuff for the big game. They had this huge display of tortilla chips right when you walk in, shaped like a football stadium. It was actually pretty cool, someone must have spent hours stacking all those bags. But then some kid ran his cart right into it and knocked like half of them down. The manager looked so mad, he was this tall guy with a really bad combover. He was wearing this bright red tie that totally clashed with his shirt. It reminded me of my old middle school principal, Mr. Henderson. He always wore these awful ties with little cartoon characters on them... \n\nBut no, I don't think I'm moving slower or anything. And I'm definitely not restless or fidgety. I'm too tired to be restless.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt down or depressed most of the day, nearly every day, since this started two months ago?\n\nPatient: I don't know if I'd say most of the day... I mean, I guess I have my moments, but I wouldn't say it's all the time. Sometimes I feel okay when I'm at work, at least until it gets really slow. When the store is dead, time just drags, you know? We had this one shift last Tuesday where I think we only had like three customers the entire afternoon. One of them was this lady trying to return a blender without a receipt. She kept arguing with my manager, saying she bought it last week, but the box looked like it had been sitting in her garage for ten years. It was covered in dust and one of the corners was completely crushed. She had this tiny little dog in her purse, too, one of those Chihuahuas that just shakes constantly. It had a little rhinestone collar that kept catching the light from the fluorescent bulbs above the register. Those bulbs give me a headache sometimes, they have this constant low buzzing sound. The maintenance guy, Dave, said he put in a work order to fix them, but corporate takes forever to approve anything. He showed me the paperwork he submitted, and it was like five pages long just to change a light fixture... \n\nBut yeah, I don't think I'm depressed all day every day. Just... sometimes.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like life wasn’t worth living or had thoughts about ending your life since this started?\n\nPatient: No, no, I haven't really thought about anything like that. I mean, I guess things feel pretty pointless sometimes, but I wouldn't actually do anything to hurt myself. It's just... I don't know, it's hard to explain. Like when you're watching a movie and you already know how it ends, so you just kind of stop paying attention? I was watching this documentary the other night about deep-sea diving, and they were showing these crazy fish that live way down where there's no light at all. Some of them actually make their own light, like they have these little glowing things hanging off their heads to attract smaller fish. It's wild how things adapt like that. The guy narrating it had this really deep, soothing voice, almost like Morgan Freeman but not quite. I think he might have been British. My uncle used to do this fake British accent whenever he'd read me stories when I was a kid. He had this whole routine where he'd pretend to be a proper gentleman drinking tea, but he'd always end up spilling it on himself. He was pretty clumsy, actually. He broke his ankle once just stepping off a curb outside a diner... \n\nBut no, to answer your question, I haven't had any thoughts about ending my life. Just feeling kind of empty, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt down or sad most of the day, nearly every day, for the past two months?\n\nPatient: Didn't you just ask me that? He's got this old '67 Mustang he's been fixing up for like, three years now. He finally got the engine rebuilt and painted it this really crazy metallic blue color. It looks awesome, but he's super paranoid about getting it wet. He won't even drive it if there's a puddle on the road. We went to grab burgers the other day, and he parked like a mile away from the restaurant just so no one would park next to him and ding his doors. Then, of course, it started drizzling while we were walking back, and he totally freaked out. He actually took off his jacket and tried to cover the hood with it. It was hilarious, but he was so mad at me for laughing... \n\nSo yeah, like I said, not every day. Just off and on, you know?\n\nDoctor: Over the past two years, have you had periods where you felt down or sad for weeks at a time, even if they came and went?\n\nPatient: Two years? I saw a picture of it online once and it totally freaked me out. I don't know why a bird needs teeth on its tongue, it just seems like overkill... \n\nBut anyway, no, I haven't had anything like that going on for two years. Most of the day, every day? No, I don't think so. Like I said, it's really just off and on. Plus I'm working most days, and when you're in retail you kind of just have to keep moving, you know? Then, of course, it started drizzling while we were walking back, and he totally freaked out. He actually took off his jacket and tried to cover the hood with it. It was hilarious, but he was so mad at me for laughing... \n\nSo yeah, like I said, not every day. Just off and on, you know?\n\nDoctor: Over the past two years, have you had periods where you felt down or sad for weeks at a time, even if they came and went?\n\nPatient: Two years? Um, no, I don't think so. Not for weeks at a time, anyway. Two years ago I was actually living in this really crappy apartment above a bakery. It wasn't sad, just... loud, you know? The bakers would get in at like 3 AM and start banging these giant metal trays around. But the smell was incredible, man. Fresh bread every morning. The owner, this guy named Sal, he was huge. Like, former football player huge. He used to give me the leftover bagels at the end of the day. Most of them were pretty stale by then, so I'd take them to the park down the street and throw them to the ducks. Except one time this massive goose chased me because I guess I didn't throw the bagel fast enough. Geese are so aggressive. They have those weird little teeth on their tongues, did you know that? I saw a picture of it online once and it totally freaked me out. I don't know why a bird needs teeth on its tongue, it just seems like overkill... \n\nBut anyway, no, I haven't had anything like that going on for two years. Just recently, really.\n\nDoctor: Over the past two months, have you had days when you felt down or sad for most of the day, nearly every day, even if it wasn't constant throughout the entire day?\n\nPatient: Most of the day, every day? We had this huge clearance event last week and this one kid came in wanting these specific neon green sneakers. We didn't have his size, and his mom threw an absolute fit. She actually chucked a shoehorn at my manager. Have you ever seen those really long metal shoehorns we use? They're basically weapons. \n\nMy manager, Dave, he just stood there and took it. Dave's a really chill guy, he used to play bass in a reggae band back in the 90s. He still wears his hair in these tiny little dreadlocks that he ties back with a rubber band. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Most of the day? I mean, maybe a few times, but not really. It's not like a whole day thing. Usually I can just shake it off if I go for a walk or something. I actually walked all the way down to the river yesterday after my shift. It's a pretty long walk from the mall, probably like three miles, but the weather was nice. There's this one spot under the old bridge where people leave all this crazy graffiti. Some of it's really good, like actual art. There was this huge mural of a tiger, but someone had painted over the eyes with bright pink spray paint. It looked kind of creepy, to be honest. 

While I was looking at it, this stray dog came up to me. He looked like a mix between a terrier and maybe a beagle? He had these huge floppy ears but wiry hair. I tried to give him a piece of a granola bar I had in my pocket, but he just sniffed it and ran off. Dogs are funny like that. My aunt used to have a poodle that would only eat chicken if you cut it into perfect little squares. If it was shredded, she wouldn't touch it. My aunt would spend like twenty minutes every night just chopping up chicken breast for this dog. She even had a special knife just for the dog's food. I always thought that was a little much, you know? 

But anyway... what did you ask? Most of the day? Yeah, no, I wouldn't say that. It just comes and goes.
